  output ports: result[64]

An unsigned design operand as wide as its port has no exact representation on this block.
  timing: the block is combinational; `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied (no clock port).

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_medium': channel width 300, device koios_medium, seed 1) has a critical path of 12.4624 ns and uses 219 DSP tiles of the device's 770, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.53973e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.18888e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in a different my_fir instance (endpoint group $dffe~788, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 12.297 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Observed across 12 timing reports through 2026-09-20; block, clock-to-Q and packing rows are from the architecture file and exact; wire and adder rows are sampled ranges with the median in parentheses.

element on a pathns
block, any mode2.14
wire, block to block0.96 to 2.22 (median 1.17)
wire, register to first block1.0 to 3.82 (median 2.25)
wire, last block to register0.83 to 2.67 (median 2.25)
wire, block into soft logic0.75 to 3.67 (median 1.48)
soft adder level, cell plus wire to the next0.65 to 2.57 (median 1.08)
clock-to-Q0.06

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

TIER: T1 | T2 | NONE
REGIONS: <comma-separated region id(s)> # one id for a direct swap; N ids for a fusion
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal/module the terms feed> # or NONE for a single region
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, a single integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, a single integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage your rewrite leaves, in ns, a single number>
MAPPING: <which operands map to which of the mode's ports>
PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout[27:0] sum inside each my_fir module (stereovision2.v:1486-1492, 1542-1548, 1602-1607, 1661-1666, 1716-1722, 1775-1780, 1833-1838); every listed product is a term of that one enabled accumulator register and has no other reader
BLOCKS: 132
TILES: 228
STAGE_NS: 8.1
MAPPING: per my_fir instance (din/n_delay_regN are 16-bit unsigned → zero-extended to ax/bx[18]; coefficients are positive constants ≤255 → ay/by[19]); each block's result[27:0] is a summand of dout.

  • my_fir_f1 (coef 29,101,15,235,15,101,29), 4 blocks: u0(ax=din, ay=29, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=101); u1(ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=15, bx=n_delay_reg3, by=235); u2(ax=n_delay_reg4, ay=15, bx=n_delay_reg5, by=101); u3(ax=n_delay_reg6, ay=29, bx=0, by=0).
  • my_fir_f2 and my_fir_h2 (coef 4,42,163,255,163,42,4), 3 blocks: u0(ax=din, ay=4, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=42); u1(ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=163, bx=n_delay_reg3, by=255); u2(ax=n_delay_reg4, ay=163, bx=n_delay_reg5, by=42); the seventh term n_delay_reg6*4 stays the wire shift {10'b0, n_delay_reg6, 2'b0} it already is and enters the adder tree as the fourth summand.
  • my_fir_f3 (12,77,148,148,77,12), 3 blocks: u0(ax=din, ay=12, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=77); u1(ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=148, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=148); u2(ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=77, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=12).
  • my_fir_h1 (15,25,193,193,25,15), 3 blocks: u0(ax=din, ay=15, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=25); u1(ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=193, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=193); u2(ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=25, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=15).
  • my_fir_h3 and my_fir_h4 (9,56,109,109,56,9), 3 blocks each: u0(ax=din, ay=9, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=56); u1(ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=109, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=109); u2(ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=56, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=9).
    Every ax/ay/bx/by is driven; only u3 of my_fir_f1 has a tied-off term (bx=18'b0, by=19'b0). 22 blocks per h_fltr instance × 6 instances = 132.

PLAN:

What changes. In each of the 7 my_fir module bodies, the product terms of the dout expression are replaced by comb_sop_2_18 instances at module scope (combinational, so they must live outside the always block), and the always block keeps dout <= <2-level tree of block results> under the same if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1) guard. Concretely for the 3-block modules: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + u2_r[27:0]; for f1: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + u3_r[27:0]); for f2/h2: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + {10'b0, n_delay_reg6, 2'b0});.

Live inputs/outputs preserved. din, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched — the delay registers keep their enable and still feed each other; the blocks only take copies of them as operands (operands are exempt from observability). dout remains the same 28-bit enabled register with the same latency, so vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* → steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:514-521) sees identical values on identical cycles. No register is moved, deleted or added, so module latency and the new_data_rdy gating are unchanged. n_delay_reg3 in f3/h1/h3/h4 keeps its role as a pure pipeline stage (its coefficient is 0 in the source).

Exactness. din and the delay registers are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extend, allowed. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports → exact. In the baseline the whole RHS is evaluated at 32-bit width, so no product is truncated before the sum; the fused sum is therefore bit-identical. Largest possible sum is f2/h2: 65535 × (4+42+163+255+163+42+4) = 44,105,055 < 2^28, so the 28-bit dout never wraps and the low-28-bit slices of the block results add to exactly the baseline value; even if it did wrap, truncation commutes with the additions. Reassociating the sum (6-deep chain → 2-level tree) is exact two's-complement integer addition. No new operation or datatype is introduced; this is T2 only because the reduction is re-associated and two ×4 shifts in f2/h2 change form (one folded into a block as a real product of the same expression, one left as the shift it is).

Delay accounting. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — the mode has no addend or cascade port, and a partial sum (up to ~26 bits) does not fit on ax[18]/bx[18]/ay[19]/by[19], so blocks cannot be chained and are all in parallel. Rewritten stage, from the source registers to dout: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register→block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft-logic wire 1.48 + 2 soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns (medians), versus the baseline slowest stage 12.4624 ns. What leaves the stage: one hard multiply feeding a 6-deep soft adder chain plus the final LUT is replaced by one block feeding a 2-level tree — four adder levels and the LUT come off the path, and the second product in each block is free arithmetic inside the block (rule 4). This is the slowest stage, and it is the shape of the second-slowest stage too (endpoint $dffe~788, 12.297 ns, a different my_fir instance), which is why all 7 module types × 6 instances are converted rather than a subset — converting only the one critical instance would leave the period at 12.297 ns and buy nothing (rule 1). After the rewrite the next candidates are the fltr_compute d_out_tmp stages (7 registered 20-bit values, 6 carry-chain adder levels, no multiply: ≈0.06 + ~2.3 wire + ~5 ≈ 7.5 ns) and steer_fltr (≤4 adder levels), both below 8.1 ns, so the expected new period is ≈8.1-8.6 ns.

Area accounting. 132 blocks, each taking its DSP tile alone. The 246 my_fir multiplies they replace occupied at least 123 tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode ("one or two to a tile"), so the planned count is 219 − 123 + 132 = 228 tiles (+9 over baseline), area ≈ 9.54e7 + 9×253779 ≈ 9.77e7, giving ADP ≈ 9.77e7 × 8.1 ≈ 7.9e8 against the baseline 1.18888e9 (−33%). I do not assume more than the minimum is freed (rule 6). Worst case, if the packer frees no tile at all, the design uses 351 of 770 tiles, area 1.29e8, ADP ≈ 1.05e9 — still below baseline, so the rewrite lowers ADP under both the expected and the pessimistic packing. Soft-logic savings (four adder levels × 28 bits × 42 instances) are real but not counted.

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  • All 42 fltr_compute ids (f1 859-865; f2 890-894; f3 928-934; h1 959-965; h2 993-997; h3 1030-1036; h4 1065-1071): each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 (e.g. stereovision2.v:859-865) and only the registered values are reduced into d_out_tmp on the following clock. The products therefore do not feed a common reduction within one stage; fusing two of them into one block would delete the q registers that sit between the multiply and the sum, changing the module's pipeline behaviour (register-control rule). Keeping the q registers and driving them from pair-sum blocks is possible in principle, but that stage is not the slowest one — the multiply→q stage is short and the q→d_out_tmp stage contains no multiply, so no soft logic would leave the critical stage, and it would cost ~126 extra tiles for zero delay benefit (rule 1: paid for in tiles alone). Left alone.
  • The two power-of-two terms in my_fir_f2/h2 that are not in the candidate list (din*4 at 1542/1716, n_delay_reg6*4 at 1548/1722): din*4 is folded into block u0 as a genuine product of the same expression (it costs nothing — that block's second term would otherwise be tied off); n_delay_reg6*4 stays a wire shift, because putting it in a block would cost a fourth tile per instance without reducing the tree depth below 2 levels.

Risks. (1) The tile delta depends on how the baseline packer shared two_mult_18x19 tiles between my_fir and fltr multiplies; if fewer than 123 tiles are freed the tile count rises toward 351 — still within the device's 770 and still ADP-positive, but less so. (2) The wire figures are sampled medians; with placement at the upper end of the block→soft-logic and adder-level ranges the rewritten stage could reach ~10-11 ns, which still beats 12.4624 ns but shrinks the gain. (3) Yosys must not re-decompose the tree or re-infer multipliers — the block instances are explicit, so the only soft arithmetic left in the stage is the 2-level 28-bit tree. (4) If some third stage outside my_fir/fltr_compute/steer_fltr sits above 8.1 ns in the baseline, the period floor is set there and the gain is smaller; the two reported slowest stages are both my_fir, which is the evidence this plan rests on.

    Every ax/ay/bx/by is driven; only u3 of my_fir_f1 has a tied-off term (bx=18'b0, by=19'b0). 22 blocks per h_fltr instance × 6 instances = 132.

PLAN:

What changes. In each of the 7 my_fir module bodies, the product terms of the dout expression are replaced by comb_sop_2_18 instances at module scope (combinational, so they must live outside the always block), and the always block keeps dout <= <2-level tree of block results> under the same if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1) guard. Concretely for the 3-block modules: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + u2_r[27:0]; for f1: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + u3_r[27:0]); for f2/h2: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + {10'b0, n_delay_reg6, 2'b0});.

Live inputs/outputs preserved. din, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched — the delay registers keep their enable and still feed each other; the blocks only take copies of them as operands (operands are exempt from observability). dout remains the same 28-bit enabled register with the same latency, so vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* → steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:514-521) sees identical values on identical cycles. No register is moved, deleted or added, so module latency and the new_data_rdy gating are unchanged. n_delay_reg3 in f3/h1/h3/h4 keeps its role as a pure pipeline stage (its coefficient is 0 in the source).

Exactness. din and the delay registers are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extend, allowed. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports → exact. In the baseline the whole RHS is evaluated at 32-bit width, so no product is truncated before the sum; the fused sum is therefore bit-identical. Largest possible sum is f2/h2: 65535 × (4+42+163+255+163+42+4) = 44,105,055 < 2^28, so the 28-bit dout never wraps and the low-28-bit slices of the block results add to exactly the baseline value; even if it did wrap, truncation commutes with the additions. Reassociating the sum (6-deep chain → 2-level tree) is exact two's-complement integer addition. No new operation or datatype is introduced; this is T2 only because the reduction is re-associated and two ×4 shifts in f2/h2 change form (one folded into a block as a real product of the same expression, one left as the shift it is).

Delay accounting. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — the mode has no addend or cascade port, and a partial sum (up to ~26 bits) does not fit on ax[18]/bx[18]/ay[19]/by[19], so blocks cannot be chained and are all in parallel. Rewritten stage, from the source registers to dout: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register→block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft-logic wire 1.48 + 2 soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns (medians), versus the baseline slowest stage 12.4624 ns. What leaves the stage: one hard multiply feeding a 6-deep soft adder chain plus the final LUT is replaced by one block feeding a 2-level tree — four adder levels and the LUT come off the path, and the second product in each block is free arithmetic inside the block (rule 4). This is the slowest stage, and it is the shape of the second-slowest stage too (endpoint $dffe~788, 12.297 ns, a different my_fir instance), which is why all 7 module types × 6 instances are converted rather than a subset — converting only the one critical instance would leave the period at 12.297 ns and buy nothing (rule 1). After the rewrite the next candidates are the fltr_compute d_out_tmp stages (7 registered 20-bit values, 6 carry-chain adder levels, no multiply: ≈0.06 + ~2.3 wire + ~5 ≈ 7.5 ns) and steer_fltr (≤4 adder levels), both below 8.1 ns, so the expected new period is ≈8.1-8.6 ns.

Area accounting. 132 blocks, each taking its DSP tile alone. The 246 my_fir multiplies they replace occupied at least 123 tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode ("one or two to a tile"), so the planned count is 219 − 123 + 132 = 228 tiles (+9 over baseline), area ≈ 9.54e7 + 9×253779 ≈ 9.77e7, giving ADP ≈ 9.77e7 × 8.1 ≈ 7.9e8 against the baseline 1.18888e9 (−33%). I do not assume more than the minimum is freed (rule 6). Worst case, if the packer frees no tile at all, the design uses 351 of 770 tiles, area 1.29e8, ADP ≈ 1.05e9 — still below baseline, so the rewrite lowers ADP under both the expected and the pessimistic packing. Soft-logic savings (four adder levels × 28 bits × 42 instances) are real but not counted.

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  • All 42 fltr_compute ids (f1 859-865; f2 890-894; f3 928-934; h1 959-965; h2 993-997; h3 1030-1036; h4 1065-1071): each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 (e.g. stereovision2.v:859-865) and only the registered values are reduced into d_out_tmp on the following clock. The products therefore do not feed a common reduction within one stage; fusing two of them into one block would delete the q registers that sit between the multiply and the sum, changing the module's pipeline behaviour (register-control rule). Keeping the q registers and driving them from pair-sum blocks is possible in principle, but that stage is not the slowest one — the multiply→q stage is short and the q→d_out_tmp stage contains no multiply, so no soft logic would leave the critical stage, and it would cost ~126 extra tiles for zero delay benefit (rule 1: paid for in tiles alone). Left alone.
  • The two power-of-two terms in my_fir_f2/h2 that are not in the candidate list (din*4 at 1542/1716, n_delay_reg6*4 at 1548/1722): din*4 is folded into block u0 as a genuine product of the same expression (it costs nothing — that block's second term would otherwise be tied off); n_delay_reg6*4 stays a wire shift, because putting it in a block would cost a fourth tile per instance without reducing the tree depth below 2 levels.

Risks. (1) The tile delta depends on how the baseline packer shared two_mult_18x19 tiles between my_fir and fltr multiplies; if fewer than 123 tiles are freed the tile count rises toward 351 — still within the device's 770 and still ADP-positive, but less so. (2) The wire figures are sampled medians; with placement at the upper end of the block→soft-logic and adder-level ranges the rewritten stage could reach ~10-11 ns, which still beats 12.4624 ns but shrinks the gain. (3) Yosys must not re-decompose the tree or re-infer multipliers — the block instances are explicit, so the only soft arithmetic left in the stage is the 2-level 28-bit tree. (4) If some third stage outside my_fir/fltr_compute/steer_fltr sits above 8.1 ns in the baseline, the period floor is set there and the gain is smaller; the two reported slowest stages are both my_fir, which is the evidence this plan rests on.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verified against /work/stereovision2.v:

  • Term coverage / no fabrication (c): The 41 listed $mul ids map exactly onto the source terms — f1 7 terms (stereovision2.v:1486-1492, coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29), f2/h2 5 $mul + 2 shift-by-4 terms (1542-1548, 1716-1722), f3/h1/h3/h4 6 terms each with n_delay_reg3 legitimately absent (COEF3 = 0 at lines 1564, 1620, 1737, 1795). Each planned block operand pair corresponds to a distinct existing product of the same dout accumulator; none is duplicated or invented. Folding the existing din * 4 (line 1542/1716) into u0's otherwise-idle second slot is a restructuring of a term that exists in the source, not a fabricated product.
  • Shared reduction: All terms in each module feed the single enabled 28-bit dout register; no other reader. Correct.
  • Function (a): Operands are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B = 16, line 1451), coefficients positive ≤255, so zero-extension into ax/bx[18] and positive constants on ay/by[19] are exact. The assignment context is 28 bits and addition mod 2^28 is associative, so the 6-deep chain → 2-level tree is bit-identical; the max-sum bound (65535×673 < 2^28) confirms no wrap anyway. Delay-register chain, enable, and latency are untouched, so vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* → steer_fltr (lines 514-521) is unchanged.
  • No new op/datatype (b): int32 multiply-accumulate already present; the mode introduces nothing new.
  • Not a mode island (d): Every block's result[27:0] is a live summand of dout, which drives steer_fltr. f1's u3 is half-padded only because that module has an odd (7) term count; its live half carries the real n_delay_reg6 * 29 term, so it is not a constant-fed or dead block.
  • Exclusion reasoning checked: the fltr_compute_* products really are captured in q1..q7 registers before the reduction (e.g. lines 859-867), so they do not share a single-stage reduction — a correct reason to leave them alone. h_fltr is instantiated 6 times, matching the 22 blocks × 6 = 132 count.

Remaining uncertainties (tile-sharing assumptions in the 228 estimate, wire-delay medians) are stated as risks by the plan and are not grounds for revision under the review criteria.

    Every ax/ay/bx/by is driven; only u3 of my_fir_f1 has a tied-off term (bx=18'b0, by=19'b0). 22 blocks per h_fltr instance × 6 instances = 132.

PLAN:

What changes. In each of the 7 my_fir module bodies, the product terms of the dout expression are replaced by comb_sop_2_18 instances at module scope (combinational, so they must live outside the always block), and the always block keeps dout <= <2-level tree of block results> under the same if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1) guard. Concretely for the 3-block modules: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + u2_r[27:0]; for f1: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + u3_r[27:0]); for f2/h2: dout <= (u0_r[27:0] + u1_r[27:0]) + (u2_r[27:0] + {10'b0, n_delay_reg6, 2'b0});.

Live inputs/outputs preserved. din, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched — the delay registers keep their enable and still feed each other; the blocks only take copies of them as operands (operands are exempt from observability). dout remains the same 28-bit enabled register with the same latency, so vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* → steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:514-521) sees identical values on identical cycles. No register is moved, deleted or added, so module latency and the new_data_rdy gating are unchanged. n_delay_reg3 in f3/h1/h3/h4 keeps its role as a pure pipeline stage (its coefficient is 0 in the source).

Exactness. din and the delay registers are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extend, allowed. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports → exact. In the baseline the whole RHS is evaluated at 32-bit width, so no product is truncated before the sum; the fused sum is therefore bit-identical. Largest possible sum is f2/h2: 65535 × (4+42+163+255+163+42+4) = 44,105,055 < 2^28, so the 28-bit dout never wraps and the low-28-bit slices of the block results add to exactly the baseline value; even if it did wrap, truncation commutes with the additions. Reassociating the sum (6-deep chain → 2-level tree) is exact two's-complement integer addition. No new operation or datatype is introduced; this is T2 only because the reduction is re-associated and two ×4 shifts in f2/h2 change form (one folded into a block as a real product of the same expression, one left as the shift it is).

Delay accounting. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — the mode has no addend or cascade port, and a partial sum (up to ~26 bits) does not fit on ax[18]/bx[18]/ay[19]/by[19], so blocks cannot be chained and are all in parallel. Rewritten stage, from the source registers to dout: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register→block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft-logic wire 1.48 + 2 soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns (medians), versus the baseline slowest stage 12.4624 ns. What leaves the stage: one hard multiply feeding a 6-deep soft adder chain plus the final LUT is replaced by one block feeding a 2-level tree — four adder levels and the LUT come off the path, and the second product in each block is free arithmetic inside the block (rule 4). This is the slowest stage, and it is the shape of the second-slowest stage too (endpoint $dffe~788, 12.297 ns, a different my_fir instance), which is why all 7 module types × 6 instances are converted rather than a subset — converting only the one critical instance would leave the period at 12.297 ns and buy nothing (rule 1). After the rewrite the next candidates are the fltr_compute d_out_tmp stages (7 registered 20-bit values, 6 carry-chain adder levels, no multiply: ≈0.06 + ~2.3 wire + ~5 ≈ 7.5 ns) and steer_fltr (≤4 adder levels), both below 8.1 ns, so the expected new period is ≈8.1-8.6 ns.

Area accounting. 132 blocks, each taking its DSP tile alone. The 246 my_fir multiplies they replace occupied at least 123 tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode ("one or two to a tile"), so the planned count is 219 − 123 + 132 = 228 tiles (+9 over baseline), area ≈ 9.54e7 + 9×253779 ≈ 9.77e7, giving ADP ≈ 9.77e7 × 8.1 ≈ 7.9e8 against the baseline 1.18888e9 (−33%). I do not assume more than the minimum is freed (rule 6). Worst case, if the packer frees no tile at all, the design uses 351 of 770 tiles, area 1.29e8, ADP ≈ 1.05e9 — still below baseline, so the rewrite lowers ADP under both the expected and the pessimistic packing. Soft-logic savings (four adder levels × 28 bits × 42 instances) are real but not counted.

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  • All 42 fltr_compute ids (f1 859-865; f2 890-894; f3 928-934; h1 959-965; h2 993-997; h3 1030-1036; h4 1065-1071): each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 (e.g. stereovision2.v:859-865) and only the registered values are reduced into d_out_tmp on the following clock. The products therefore do not feed a common reduction within one stage; fusing two of them into one block would delete the q registers that sit between the multiply and the sum, changing the module's pipeline behaviour (register-control rule). Keeping the q registers and driving them from pair-sum blocks is possible in principle, but that stage is not the slowest one — the multiply→q stage is short and the q→d_out_tmp stage contains no multiply, so no soft logic would leave the critical stage, and it would cost ~126 extra tiles for zero delay benefit (rule 1: paid for in tiles alone). Left alone.
  • The two power-of-two terms in my_fir_f2/h2 that are not in the candidate list (din*4 at 1542/1716, n_delay_reg6*4 at 1548/1722): din*4 is folded into block u0 as a genuine product of the same expression (it costs nothing — that block's second term would otherwise be tied off); n_delay_reg6*4 stays a wire shift, because putting it in a block would cost a fourth tile per instance without reducing the tree depth below 2 levels.

Risks. (1) The tile delta depends on how the baseline packer shared two_mult_18x19 tiles between my_fir and fltr multiplies; if fewer than 123 tiles are freed the tile count rises toward 351 — still within the device's 770 and still ADP-positive, but less so. (2) The wire figures are sampled medians; with placement at the upper end of the block→soft-logic and adder-level ranges the rewritten stage could reach ~10-11 ns, which still beats 12.4624 ns but shrinks the gain. (3) Yosys must not re-decompose the tree or re-infer multipliers — the block instances are explicit, so the only soft arithmetic left in the stage is the 2-level 28-bit tree. (4) If some third stage outside my_fir/fltr_compute/steer_fltr sits above 8.1 ns in the baseline, the period floor is set there and the gain is smaller; the two reported slowest stages are both my_fir, which is the evidence this plan rests on.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Call 4 · reviewer:acceptance · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 18 · out 6229 · reasoning 4097 · cache read 191188 · cache write 26389 · 92.8 s
prompt

You are an INDEPENDENT ACCEPTANCE REVIEWER — the gate of record for whether an adapted design is
a LEGITIMATE member of its benchmark suite. You did NOT plan or write this edit. Judge only what
is in front of you. Your verdict is final and fully automated; there is no human backstop, so err
toward rejecting anything you cannot positively ground.

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

response

I'll examine the actual file before judging.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → file). The witness records role my_fir_f1…h4, operation sop, int32, with the shared reduction being "the dout[27:0] sum inside each my_fir module … every listed product is a term of that one enabled accumulator register." That is exactly what remains: each of the seven modules is still a 7-tap transversal FIR — the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain is untouched (stereovision2.v:1490-1495, 1552-1557, 1617-1622, 1682-1687, and the h2/h3/h4 equivalents), output_data_ready still pulses on new_data_rdy, and dout is still the same 28-bit enabled accumulator register. No control-path logic was recruited, so no (c) role break.

Real reduction, not a fabricated dot product. Every fused pair is two taps of the same module's dout sum, never a cross-module or cross-reduction fusion:

  • my_fir_f1 (:1477-1484): 7 taps → 4 blocks, coefficients COEF0_b..COEF6_b matched to din, reg1..reg6 in tap order.
  • my_fir_f3/my_fir_h1/my_fir_h3/my_fir_h4 (:1606-1611, :1671-1676, :1797-1802, :1861-1866): the six non-zero taps, with COEF3=0 (:1577, :1639, :1768, :1832) omitted — precisely the six products the pristine expression contained.
  • my_fir_f2/my_fir_h2 (:1541-1546, :1733-1738): six taps in blocks, and {10'b0, n_delay_reg6, 2'b0} is exactly n_delay_reg6 * 4 = COEF6_c/COEF6_2 (:1513, :1706) — the shift the pristine tap already synthesized to, not a stub.

Operand pairings are tap-to-coefficient correct in every module; no operand is invented to fill arity. sop3's tied-off second lane in f1 (:1483-1484) is odd-tap padding on a 7-term sum, not a fabricated operand — so no (a).

Result drives the original consumers. All four/three block results are summed into dout (:1498, :1560, :1625, :1690, :1816, :1880), which leaves each module into vidin_out_temp_* (:514-520), is registered at :527-553, and feeds steer_fltr at :521, producing real_z/imag_z/real_p/imag_p/real_n/imag_n. Nothing is sunk or ignored — no (b) mode island.

Numerics hold. Ports are zero-extended ({2'b00, din} into an 18-bit port, 19-bit positive coefficients ≤255), so the active unsigned/positive coefficient sets (:1440-1449, :1507-1513, :1574-1580, :1635-1644, :1765-1771, :1829-1835) stay in range; truncating each block to [27:0] and summing is bit-identical to the pristine 28-bit truncation of the full 7-term sum (mod-2^28 addition is associative).

T2, so no variant axis applies — the manifest introduces no operations or datatypes, and the datatype/precision of the region is unchanged, so (d) is not in play.

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_medium': channel width 300, device koios_medium, seed 1) has a critical path of 12.4624 ns and uses 219 DSP tiles of the device's 770, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.53973e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.18888e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in a different my_fir instance (endpoint group $dffe~788, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 12.297 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum of each my_fir_* module (one 28-bit accumulator per module instance: dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... + (n_delay_reg6*C6)); all listed products already feed that one sum

MAPPING:
Per my_fir instance, first form the symmetric-tap pre-sums (all operands are 16-bit unsigned, so each pre-sum is 17-bit unsigned, max 2*(2^16-1)=131070 <= 131071 = largest positive on an 18-bit signed port; zero-extend to 18 bits):
s0 = din + n_delay_reg6, s1 = n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg5, s2 = n_delay_reg2 + n_delay_reg4. Coefficients are positive constants <= 255, zero-extended onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports.

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd29, bx=s1, by=19'd101 (regions $154,$165 and $155,$163)
    B: ax=s2, ay=19'd15, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd235 (regions $157,$161 and $159)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_f2 (4,42,163,255,163,42,4), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd4, bx=s1, by=19'd42; B: ax=s2, ay=19'd163, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd255
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_h2 (same coefs as f2), 2 blocks, identical mapping.
  • my_fir_f3 (12,77,148,-,148,77,12), 1 block + 1 kept 18x19 multiply:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd12, bx=s1, by=19'd77; p2 = s2 * 148 (left as *, regions $187/$189)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + p2[27:0];
  • my_fir_h1 (15,25,193,-,193,25,15): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd15, bx=s1, by=19'd25; p2 = s2 * 193.
  • my_fir_h3, my_fir_h4 (9,56,109,-,109,56,9): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd9, bx=s1, by=19'd56; p2 = s2 * 109.

No block has an unused input port: every ax/ay/bx/by carries a real operand (f1/f2/h2 block B uses n_delay_reg3 and its coefficient on the b pair); no tie-offs are needed anywhere.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the measured 132-block rewrite. That rewrite mapped the seven raw products of each FIR onto 4 (or 3) blocks and then summed the block results in a two-level soft adder tree; the measured path was block -> 3-bit adder -> 1.000 ns wire -> 28-bit carry chain -> LUT -> dffe. Every my_fir here is an exactly symmetric FIR (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3: 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4: 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). Folding din*C + n_delay_reg6*C into (din+n_delay_reg6)*C halves the product count (7 -> 4, 6 -> 3), so each FIR needs 2 blocks instead of 4, and one soft adder level after the blocks instead of two. That is 22 blocks/instance -> 10 blocks + 1 kept multiply per instance: 60 blocks and ~72 fewer DSP tiles than the measured rewrite (188 -> 128).

Blocks and tiles. 60 blocks, each taking a tile alone. The 24 leftover s2*C2 multiplies in f3/h1/h3/h4 are exactly the 18x19 form the baseline already uses, so the packer places them one or two to a tile: 12 tiles if paired, 24 if not. Non-my_fir tiles are unchanged at 56 (the fltr_compute 9x9 group plus the rest, measured as 188-132). Total 128 tiles (140 worst case), against 219 in the unmodified design and 770 on the device. Estimated used area 128*253779 + ~1250 clusters ~= 6.7e7, versus 8.24e7 measured for the previous rewrite.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The mode has no chain port and the pre-sums are 17 bits (they cannot be fed back through an ax port), so blocks are never in series. The stage I touch, from the table medians: clock-to-Q 0.06 + wire reg->pre-adder ~1.0 + 17-bit carry chain 0.45 (0.049 + 14x0.026 + 0.035, as in the measured trace) + wire adder->block ~1.2 + block 2.14 + wire block->soft logic 0.75 + 28-bit carry chain 0.72 + wire adder->LUT 2.12 + LUT 0.153 + setup 0.02 = ~8.6 ns. Against the 8.47 ns the previous rewrite measured this is +0.15 ns (one 16-bit pre-add level and its wire enter the stage; one whole 28-bit adder level, its wire, and the 3-bit adder leave). Against the 12.46 ns baseline it is -3.9 ns. This stage is the slowest one: the measured critical path of the previous rewrite was a my_fir stage of this shape, so every non-my_fir stage is already at or below 8.47 ns; the design's period becomes max(8.6, next stage <= 8.47) = ~8.6.

Why this lowers the product. ~6.7e7 x 8.6 = ~5.8e8, against the previous 6.98e8 (-17%) and the baseline 1.18888e9 (-51%). The trade is explicit and goes against relationship 1 in one direction: I accept ~0.15 ns on the slowest stage to remove 60 DSP tiles (~1.5e7 area, ~9 CLBs per tile). The downside is bounded: even at 9.2 ns and 140 tiles the product is 6.5e8, still below 6.98e8. Fewer DSP tiles should also relieve the placement spread that produced the 2.12 ns adder->LUT wire, so the delay may come in at or below 8.47.

Exactness. All operands are unsigned and non-negative: din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit, coefficients are positive literals <= 255. Bounds: the largest coefficient sum is f2/h2 at 673, so the full sum is at most 65535*673 = 4.41e7 < 2^28 - the original 28-bit dout never wraps, and the fused sum is the same integer, so result[27:0] and the final 28-bit add reproduce it bit for bit. Pre-sums are 17-bit and zero-extend onto the 18-bit signed ax/bx ports (131070 <= 131071, the port's largest positive); coefficients zero-extend onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the original (each q/product is carried at full width), so a sum truncated after is identical. Block results are positive and far below 2^63, so the signed 64-bit result is exact.

Preservation of inputs, outputs, consumers, registers. The shift chain n_delay_reg1..6 <= ... and output_data_ready are untouched; s0/s1/s2 are new combinational wires read only by the blocks/kept multiply, so nothing else loses visibility of a value (Observability). din and the delay registers keep their own drivers and readers - the blocks only take copies of them as operands. No register goes inside a block: dout stays the same 28-bit register inside if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1), driven by the one soft add of the two 28-bit slices, so its enable, width and the module's latency are unchanged (Register control, relationship 5: no register is moved or added). dout still drives vidin_out_temp_* into the parent's enabled vidin_out_reg_*, which feeds steer_fltr unchanged. comb_sop_2_18 is instantiated as a black box with exactly the port names ax, ay, bx, by, result.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The pre-adds are integer adds of values the module already computes, in the same unsigned integer datatype; the folding is algebraic re-association of one reduction (T2).

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  1. All 42 fltr_compute_ candidates* (f1 $44-$50, f2 $59-$63, f3 $72-$78, h1 $86-$92, h2 $101-$105, h3 $114-$120, h4 $128-$134): left alone. These are 8-bit-by-small-constant multiplies that the packer already places four to a DSP tile (54 tiles for all of them), while a comb_sop_2_18 block takes a tile alone - fusing them in pairs would turn ~54 tiles into ~168 (relationship 6). They also sit on a stage that is not the slowest (the measured 8.47 ns path is in my_fir, and these stages did not appear), so by relationship 1 the conversion could not shorten the period and would be paid in tiles alone. Additionally each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 before the shared d_out_tmp sum, so fusing a pair would delete a register's behaviour or force it to be rebuilt (Register control).
  2. The middle-tap products of my_fir_f3 ($187/$189), my_fir_h1 ($202/$204), my_fir_h3 ($230/$232), my_fir_h4 ($243/$245): restructured by the same symmetry folding (two products become one), but the resulting single product s2*C2 is left as a plain multiply rather than given a block. These four modules have only three folded products; a block for the third product would use a whole tile for one multiply, whereas the kept * maps to the baseline's two_mult_18x19 mode and packs one or two to a tile. Its delay is the same 2.14 ns as a block, so the stage is unaffected.

Risks. (a) If the packer does not pair the 24 kept multiplies, tiles land at 140 rather than 128; the product is still below the previous result. (b) The pre-adder adds one carry-chain level to the my_fir stage; if routing to the blocks is worse than the 1.2 ns the previous build showed, the stage could reach ~9 ns - still a net win, but the margin narrows. (c) The 28-bit final add must be written on explicit [27:0] slices of the 64-bit result ports so synthesis builds a 28-bit chain and not a wider one. (d) Some non-my_fir stage may sit just under 8.47 ns and become the new critical path, in which case the delay gain saturates but the tile saving still stands.

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum of each my_fir_* module (one 28-bit accumulator per module instance: dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... + (n_delay_reg6*C6)); all listed products already feed that one sum
BLOCKS: 60
TILES: 128
STAGE_NS: 8.6
MAPPING:
Per my_fir instance, first form the symmetric-tap pre-sums (all operands are 16-bit unsigned, so each pre-sum is 17-bit unsigned, max 2*(2^16-1)=131070 <= 131071 = largest positive on an 18-bit signed port; zero-extend to 18 bits):
s0 = din + n_delay_reg6, s1 = n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg5, s2 = n_delay_reg2 + n_delay_reg4. Coefficients are positive constants <= 255, zero-extended onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports.

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd29, bx=s1, by=19'd101 (regions $154,$165 and $155,$163)
    B: ax=s2, ay=19'd15, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd235 (regions $157,$161 and $159)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_f2 (4,42,163,255,163,42,4), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd4, bx=s1, by=19'd42; B: ax=s2, ay=19'd163, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd255
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_h2 (same coefs as f2), 2 blocks, identical mapping.
  • my_fir_f3 (12,77,148,-,148,77,12), 1 block + 1 kept 18x19 multiply:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd12, bx=s1, by=19'd77; p2 = s2 * 148 (left as *, regions $187/$189)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + p2[27:0];
  • my_fir_h1 (15,25,193,-,193,25,15): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd15, bx=s1, by=19'd25; p2 = s2 * 193.
  • my_fir_h3, my_fir_h4 (9,56,109,-,109,56,9): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd9, bx=s1, by=19'd56; p2 = s2 * 109.

No block has an unused input port: every ax/ay/bx/by carries a real operand (f1/f2/h2 block B uses n_delay_reg3 and its coefficient on the b pair); no tie-offs are needed anywhere.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the measured 132-block rewrite. That rewrite mapped the seven raw products of each FIR onto 4 (or 3) blocks and then summed the block results in a two-level soft adder tree; the measured path was block -> 3-bit adder -> 1.000 ns wire -> 28-bit carry chain -> LUT -> dffe. Every my_fir here is an exactly symmetric FIR (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3: 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4: 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). Folding din*C + n_delay_reg6*C into (din+n_delay_reg6)*C halves the product count (7 -> 4, 6 -> 3), so each FIR needs 2 blocks instead of 4, and one soft adder level after the blocks instead of two. That is 22 blocks/instance -> 10 blocks + 1 kept multiply per instance: 60 blocks and ~72 fewer DSP tiles than the measured rewrite (188 -> 128).

Blocks and tiles. 60 blocks, each taking a tile alone. The 24 leftover s2*C2 multiplies in f3/h1/h3/h4 are exactly the 18x19 form the baseline already uses, so the packer places them one or two to a tile: 12 tiles if paired, 24 if not. Non-my_fir tiles are unchanged at 56 (the fltr_compute 9x9 group plus the rest, measured as 188-132). Total 128 tiles (140 worst case), against 219 in the unmodified design and 770 on the device. Estimated used area 128*253779 + ~1250 clusters ~= 6.7e7, versus 8.24e7 measured for the previous rewrite.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The mode has no chain port and the pre-sums are 17 bits (they cannot be fed back through an ax port), so blocks are never in series. The stage I touch, from the table medians: clock-to-Q 0.06 + wire reg->pre-adder ~1.0 + 17-bit carry chain 0.45 (0.049 + 14x0.026 + 0.035, as in the measured trace) + wire adder->block ~1.2 + block 2.14 + wire block->soft logic 0.75 + 28-bit carry chain 0.72 + wire adder->LUT 2.12 + LUT 0.153 + setup 0.02 = ~8.6 ns. Against the 8.47 ns the previous rewrite measured this is +0.15 ns (one 16-bit pre-add level and its wire enter the stage; one whole 28-bit adder level, its wire, and the 3-bit adder leave). Against the 12.46 ns baseline it is -3.9 ns. This stage is the slowest one: the measured critical path of the previous rewrite was a my_fir stage of this shape, so every non-my_fir stage is already at or below 8.47 ns; the design's period becomes max(8.6, next stage <= 8.47) = ~8.6.

Why this lowers the product. ~6.7e7 x 8.6 = ~5.8e8, against the previous 6.98e8 (-17%) and the baseline 1.18888e9 (-51%). The trade is explicit and goes against relationship 1 in one direction: I accept ~0.15 ns on the slowest stage to remove 60 DSP tiles (~1.5e7 area, ~9 CLBs per tile). The downside is bounded: even at 9.2 ns and 140 tiles the product is 6.5e8, still below 6.98e8. Fewer DSP tiles should also relieve the placement spread that produced the 2.12 ns adder->LUT wire, so the delay may come in at or below 8.47.

Exactness. All operands are unsigned and non-negative: din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit, coefficients are positive literals <= 255. Bounds: the largest coefficient sum is f2/h2 at 673, so the full sum is at most 65535*673 = 4.41e7 < 2^28 - the original 28-bit dout never wraps, and the fused sum is the same integer, so result[27:0] and the final 28-bit add reproduce it bit for bit. Pre-sums are 17-bit and zero-extend onto the 18-bit signed ax/bx ports (131070 <= 131071, the port's largest positive); coefficients zero-extend onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the original (each q/product is carried at full width), so a sum truncated after is identical. Block results are positive and far below 2^63, so the signed 64-bit result is exact.

Preservation of inputs, outputs, consumers, registers. The shift chain n_delay_reg1..6 <= ... and output_data_ready are untouched; s0/s1/s2 are new combinational wires read only by the blocks/kept multiply, so nothing else loses visibility of a value (Observability). din and the delay registers keep their own drivers and readers - the blocks only take copies of them as operands. No register goes inside a block: dout stays the same 28-bit register inside if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1), driven by the one soft add of the two 28-bit slices, so its enable, width and the module's latency are unchanged (Register control, relationship 5: no register is moved or added). dout still drives vidin_out_temp_* into the parent's enabled vidin_out_reg_*, which feeds steer_fltr unchanged. comb_sop_2_18 is instantiated as a black box with exactly the port names ax, ay, bx, by, result.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The pre-adds are integer adds of values the module already computes, in the same unsigned integer datatype; the folding is algebraic re-association of one reduction (T2).

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  1. All 42 fltr_compute_ candidates* (f1 $44-$50, f2 $59-$63, f3 $72-$78, h1 $86-$92, h2 $101-$105, h3 $114-$120, h4 $128-$134): left alone. These are 8-bit-by-small-constant multiplies that the packer already places four to a DSP tile (54 tiles for all of them), while a comb_sop_2_18 block takes a tile alone - fusing them in pairs would turn ~54 tiles into ~168 (relationship 6). They also sit on a stage that is not the slowest (the measured 8.47 ns path is in my_fir, and these stages did not appear), so by relationship 1 the conversion could not shorten the period and would be paid in tiles alone. Additionally each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 before the shared d_out_tmp sum, so fusing a pair would delete a register's behaviour or force it to be rebuilt (Register control).
  2. The middle-tap products of my_fir_f3 ($187/$189), my_fir_h1 ($202/$204), my_fir_h3 ($230/$232), my_fir_h4 ($243/$245): restructured by the same symmetry folding (two products become one), but the resulting single product s2*C2 is left as a plain multiply rather than given a block. These four modules have only three folded products; a block for the third product would use a whole tile for one multiply, whereas the kept * maps to the baseline's two_mult_18x19 mode and packs one or two to a tile. Its delay is the same 2.14 ns as a block, so the stage is unaffected.

Risks. (a) If the packer does not pair the 24 kept multiplies, tiles land at 140 rather than 128; the product is still below the previous result. (b) The pre-adder adds one carry-chain level to the my_fir stage; if routing to the blocks is worse than the 1.2 ns the previous build showed, the stage could reach ~9 ns - still a net win, but the margin narrows. (c) The 28-bit final add must be written on explicit [27:0] slices of the 64-bit result ports so synthesis builds a 28-bit chain and not a wider one. (d) Some non-my_fir stage may sit just under 8.47 ns and become the new critical path, in which case the delay gain saturates but the tile saving still stands.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verified against /work/stereovision2.v:

  • Region coverage matches the source exactly. Products per module: f1 7 (stereovision2.v:1486-1492), f2 5 mul cells (:1543-1547; the two ×4 taps at 1542/1548 are shifts), f3 6 (:1602-1607), h1 6 (:1661-1666), h2 5 (:1717-1721), h3 6 (:1775-1780), h4 6 (:1833-1838) = 41, the selected set.
  • (a) Function preserved. Every FIR is exactly symmetric (f1 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2 4,42,163,255,…; f3 12,77,148,–,148,77,12; h1 15,25,193,…; h3/h4 9,56,109,…), so din*C0 + n_delay_reg6*C0 → (din+n_delay_reg6)*C0 is plain re-association of the single dout reduction. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B = 16), pre-sums ≤ 131070 fit the 18-bit signed positive range, max coefficient sum 673 gives 65535·673 = 4.41e7 < 2^28, so no wrap and the 28-bit slice add is bit-identical. The dout register, its new_data_rdy enable, the shift chain, and output_data_ready are untouched.
  • (b) No new operation/datatype. Pre-adds are integer adds on values the module already holds; the sop mode is the target family.
  • (c) No fabricated terms. Every block's two products are real taps of the same dout sum, including f2/h2's ×4 taps, which exist in the RTL even though yosys lowered them to shifts. No port is tied to a constant operand (block B in f1/f2/h2 carries n_delay_reg3 and its coefficient).
  • (d) No mode island. Blocks feed dout → vidin_out_temp_* → the enabled vidin_out_reg_* at stereovision2.v:527-553 → steer_fltr (:521), the live baseline consumers. 60 blocks = 10 per h_fltr × 6 instantiations (:188-190, :195-197), consistent with the plan's counts.

Region-ID labels for the kept middle-tap multiplies in h1/h3/h4 in the "left unconverted" prose are off by one slot ($200/$202, $228/$230, $241/$243), but the MAPPING itself (s2 = n_delay_reg2 + n_delay_reg4 times C2) is correct; that is a wording slip, not a violation.

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum of each my_fir_* module (one 28-bit accumulator per module instance: dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... + (n_delay_reg6*C6)); all listed products already feed that one sum
BLOCKS: 60
TILES: 128
STAGE_NS: 8.6
MAPPING:
Per my_fir instance, first form the symmetric-tap pre-sums (all operands are 16-bit unsigned, so each pre-sum is 17-bit unsigned, max 2*(2^16-1)=131070 <= 131071 = largest positive on an 18-bit signed port; zero-extend to 18 bits):
s0 = din + n_delay_reg6, s1 = n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg5, s2 = n_delay_reg2 + n_delay_reg4. Coefficients are positive constants <= 255, zero-extended onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports.

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd29, bx=s1, by=19'd101 (regions $154,$165 and $155,$163)
    B: ax=s2, ay=19'd15, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd235 (regions $157,$161 and $159)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_f2 (4,42,163,255,163,42,4), 2 blocks:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd4, bx=s1, by=19'd42; B: ax=s2, ay=19'd163, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg3}, by=19'd255
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + B.result[27:0];
  • my_fir_h2 (same coefs as f2), 2 blocks, identical mapping.
  • my_fir_f3 (12,77,148,-,148,77,12), 1 block + 1 kept 18x19 multiply:
    A: ax=s0, ay=19'd12, bx=s1, by=19'd77; p2 = s2 * 148 (left as *, regions $187/$189)
    dout <= A.result[27:0] + p2[27:0];
  • my_fir_h1 (15,25,193,-,193,25,15): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd15, bx=s1, by=19'd25; p2 = s2 * 193.
  • my_fir_h3, my_fir_h4 (9,56,109,-,109,56,9): A: ax=s0, ay=19'd9, bx=s1, by=19'd56; p2 = s2 * 109.

No block has an unused input port: every ax/ay/bx/by carries a real operand (f1/f2/h2 block B uses n_delay_reg3 and its coefficient on the b pair); no tie-offs are needed anywhere.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the measured 132-block rewrite. That rewrite mapped the seven raw products of each FIR onto 4 (or 3) blocks and then summed the block results in a two-level soft adder tree; the measured path was block -> 3-bit adder -> 1.000 ns wire -> 28-bit carry chain -> LUT -> dffe. Every my_fir here is an exactly symmetric FIR (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3: 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4: 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). Folding din*C + n_delay_reg6*C into (din+n_delay_reg6)*C halves the product count (7 -> 4, 6 -> 3), so each FIR needs 2 blocks instead of 4, and one soft adder level after the blocks instead of two. That is 22 blocks/instance -> 10 blocks + 1 kept multiply per instance: 60 blocks and ~72 fewer DSP tiles than the measured rewrite (188 -> 128).

Blocks and tiles. 60 blocks, each taking a tile alone. The 24 leftover s2*C2 multiplies in f3/h1/h3/h4 are exactly the 18x19 form the baseline already uses, so the packer places them one or two to a tile: 12 tiles if paired, 24 if not. Non-my_fir tiles are unchanged at 56 (the fltr_compute 9x9 group plus the rest, measured as 188-132). Total 128 tiles (140 worst case), against 219 in the unmodified design and 770 on the device. Estimated used area 128*253779 + ~1250 clusters ~= 6.7e7, versus 8.24e7 measured for the previous rewrite.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The mode has no chain port and the pre-sums are 17 bits (they cannot be fed back through an ax port), so blocks are never in series. The stage I touch, from the table medians: clock-to-Q 0.06 + wire reg->pre-adder ~1.0 + 17-bit carry chain 0.45 (0.049 + 14x0.026 + 0.035, as in the measured trace) + wire adder->block ~1.2 + block 2.14 + wire block->soft logic 0.75 + 28-bit carry chain 0.72 + wire adder->LUT 2.12 + LUT 0.153 + setup 0.02 = ~8.6 ns. Against the 8.47 ns the previous rewrite measured this is +0.15 ns (one 16-bit pre-add level and its wire enter the stage; one whole 28-bit adder level, its wire, and the 3-bit adder leave). Against the 12.46 ns baseline it is -3.9 ns. This stage is the slowest one: the measured critical path of the previous rewrite was a my_fir stage of this shape, so every non-my_fir stage is already at or below 8.47 ns; the design's period becomes max(8.6, next stage <= 8.47) = ~8.6.

Why this lowers the product. ~6.7e7 x 8.6 = ~5.8e8, against the previous 6.98e8 (-17%) and the baseline 1.18888e9 (-51%). The trade is explicit and goes against relationship 1 in one direction: I accept ~0.15 ns on the slowest stage to remove 60 DSP tiles (~1.5e7 area, ~9 CLBs per tile). The downside is bounded: even at 9.2 ns and 140 tiles the product is 6.5e8, still below 6.98e8. Fewer DSP tiles should also relieve the placement spread that produced the 2.12 ns adder->LUT wire, so the delay may come in at or below 8.47.

Exactness. All operands are unsigned and non-negative: din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit, coefficients are positive literals <= 255. Bounds: the largest coefficient sum is f2/h2 at 673, so the full sum is at most 65535*673 = 4.41e7 < 2^28 - the original 28-bit dout never wraps, and the fused sum is the same integer, so result[27:0] and the final 28-bit add reproduce it bit for bit. Pre-sums are 17-bit and zero-extend onto the 18-bit signed ax/bx ports (131070 <= 131071, the port's largest positive); coefficients zero-extend onto the 19-bit signed ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the original (each q/product is carried at full width), so a sum truncated after is identical. Block results are positive and far below 2^63, so the signed 64-bit result is exact.

Preservation of inputs, outputs, consumers, registers. The shift chain n_delay_reg1..6 <= ... and output_data_ready are untouched; s0/s1/s2 are new combinational wires read only by the blocks/kept multiply, so nothing else loses visibility of a value (Observability). din and the delay registers keep their own drivers and readers - the blocks only take copies of them as operands. No register goes inside a block: dout stays the same 28-bit register inside if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1), driven by the one soft add of the two 28-bit slices, so its enable, width and the module's latency are unchanged (Register control, relationship 5: no register is moved or added). dout still drives vidin_out_temp_* into the parent's enabled vidin_out_reg_*, which feeds steer_fltr unchanged. comb_sop_2_18 is instantiated as a black box with exactly the port names ax, ay, bx, by, result.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The pre-adds are integer adds of values the module already computes, in the same unsigned integer datatype; the folding is algebraic re-association of one reduction (T2).

Candidates left unconverted, grouped by reason.

  1. All 42 fltr_compute_ candidates* (f1 $44-$50, f2 $59-$63, f3 $72-$78, h1 $86-$92, h2 $101-$105, h3 $114-$120, h4 $128-$134): left alone. These are 8-bit-by-small-constant multiplies that the packer already places four to a DSP tile (54 tiles for all of them), while a comb_sop_2_18 block takes a tile alone - fusing them in pairs would turn ~54 tiles into ~168 (relationship 6). They also sit on a stage that is not the slowest (the measured 8.47 ns path is in my_fir, and these stages did not appear), so by relationship 1 the conversion could not shorten the period and would be paid in tiles alone. Additionally each product is captured in its own register q1..q7 before the shared d_out_tmp sum, so fusing a pair would delete a register's behaviour or force it to be rebuilt (Register control).
  2. The middle-tap products of my_fir_f3 ($187/$189), my_fir_h1 ($202/$204), my_fir_h3 ($230/$232), my_fir_h4 ($243/$245): restructured by the same symmetry folding (two products become one), but the resulting single product s2*C2 is left as a plain multiply rather than given a block. These four modules have only three folded products; a block for the third product would use a whole tile for one multiply, whereas the kept * maps to the baseline's two_mult_18x19 mode and packs one or two to a tile. Its delay is the same 2.14 ns as a block, so the stage is unaffected.

Risks. (a) If the packer does not pair the 24 kept multiplies, tiles land at 140 rather than 128; the product is still below the previous result. (b) The pre-adder adds one carry-chain level to the my_fir stage; if routing to the blocks is worse than the 1.2 ns the previous build showed, the stage could reach ~9 ns - still a net win, but the margin narrows. (c) The 28-bit final add must be written on explicit [27:0] slices of the 64-bit result ports so synthesis builds a 28-bit chain and not a wider one. (d) Some non-my_fir stage may sit just under 8.47 ns and become the new critical path, in which case the delay gain saturates but the tile saving still stands.

Work iteratively IN THIS SESSION: after each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision2.v and read its output; if it does not synthesize, fix the RTL and check again. Do NOT run place-and-route or any VTR/vpr command — that is verified separately. Stop when check.sh reports it synthesizes cleanly and its cell census shows the intended arithmetic. Then state what you changed. You are one step of an automated pipeline: your edit is verified automatically and no human reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.

response

I'll start by reading the relevant parts of the file and the check script.

Yosys accepts the undeclared hard block as a blackbox cell, so no local stub is needed. Now the rewrite:

Verified: check.sh passes and a 3000-cycle random simulation (plus the all-ones saturation corner, with new_data_rdy gated) shows zero mismatches against a golden reference of the original tap sums.

What I changed (only the seven my_fir_* modules in /work/stereovision2.v):

  • Symmetric-tap folding. Each FIR has mirrored coefficients, so C0*din + C6*n_delay_reg6 became C0*(din + n_delay_reg6), and likewise for taps 1/5 and 2/4. Three new 17-bit unsigned combinational wires tap_sum_0/1/2 carry the pre-sums (max 131070, which fits the 18-bit signed ax/bx ports as a zero-extended positive value). Coefficients are zero-extended onto 19-bit coef_* wires.

  • Hard blocks. comb_sop_2_18 instances replace the folded products:

    • my_fir_f1, my_fir_f2, my_fir_h2 (7 live taps): sop_a = tap_sum_0*C0 + tap_sum_1*C1, sop_b = tap_sum_2*C2 + n_delay_reg3*C3; dout <= sop_a_result[27:0] + sop_b_result[27:0].
    • my_fir_f3, my_fir_h1, my_fir_h3, my_fir_h4 (6 live taps, C3=0): sop_a = tap_sum_0*C0 + tap_sum_1*C1; the odd third product stays a plain multiply mid_product = tap_sum_2 * coef_2; dout <= sop_a_result[27:0] + mid_product.
    • Every ax/ay/bx/by carries a real operand from the listed regions — no tie-offs, no fabricated terms.
  • Unchanged: the delay-line shift chain, output_data_ready, the 28-bit registered dout with its new_data_rdy enable, module ports, and all other modules/consumers. Coefficient values are still read from the existing `COEF* defines.

Cell census after the edit: comb_sop_2_18 × 60 (10 per FIR set, 6 sets through the hierarchy), $mul down from 444 to 276. No block stub was added to the source — yosys treats the instantiation as a blackbox, so the real hard-block library binds it.

check.sh
stereovision2.v
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

module my_fir_f1 (clk, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready, din, dout);

//coefdata=29,101,-15,-235,-15,101,29;

	//parameter `WIDTH = 5'b10000;

    input clk; 
    input[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0] din; 
    output[28 - 1:0] dout; 
    reg[28 - 1:0] dout;
	input new_data_rdy;
	output output_data_ready;
	reg output_data_ready;

    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg1;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg2;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg3;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg4;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg5;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg6;

	// symmetric taps folded before the multiply:
	// C0*din + C6*n_delay_reg6 = C0*(din + n_delay_reg6), etc. (17-bit unsigned sums)
	wire[17 - 1:0] tap_sum_0 = din + n_delay_reg6;
	wire[17 - 1:0] tap_sum_1 = n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg5;
	wire[17 - 1:0] tap_sum_2 = n_delay_reg2 + n_delay_reg4;
	wire[19 - 1:0] coef_0 = `COEF0_b;
	wire[19 - 1:0] coef_1 = `COEF1_b;
	wire[19 - 1:0] coef_2 = `COEF2_b;
	wire[19 - 1:0] coef_3 = `COEF3_b;
	wire[64 - 1:0] sop_a_result;
	wire[64 - 1:0] sop_b_result;

	comb_sop_2_18 sop_a (
		.ax({1'b0, tap_sum_0}), .ay(coef_0),
		.bx({1'b0, tap_sum_1}), .by(coef_1),
		.result(sop_a_result));

	comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (
		.ax({1'b0, tap_sum_2}), .ay(coef_2),
		.bx({2'b0, n_delay_reg3}), .by(coef_3),
		.result(sop_b_result));

    always @(posedge clk)
    begin
		if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1)
		begin
			n_delay_reg1 <= din;
			n_delay_reg2 <= n_delay_reg1;
			n_delay_reg3 <= n_delay_reg2;
			n_delay_reg4 <= n_delay_reg3;
			n_delay_reg5 <= n_delay_reg4;
			n_delay_reg6 <= n_delay_reg5;
		
			output_data_ready <= 1'b1;
			dout <= sop_a_result[28 - 1:0] + sop_b_result[28 - 1:0];
		end
		else
		begin
			output_data_ready <= 1'b0;
		end
	end
endmodule
	`define COEF0_c  4
	`define COEF1_c  42
	`define COEF2_c  163
	`define COEF3_c  255
	`define COEF4_c  163
